交易咨询业务资格：证监许可【2012】31号

10月金融数据点评

宏观 
报告日期 2026—11-16 专题报告 
   10月金融数据呈现"社融微升、信贷负增、M1 修复、M2续
降"的组合。社融存量同比增速较 9 月回升 0.1 个百分点
至7.2%，主要得益于政府债券集中发行；单月新增社融约
1.15 万亿元，同比多增逾 3000 亿元，但增量几乎全部由
政府部门贡献。金融机构新增人民币贷款-1000 亿元，同
比少增约 3200 亿元，票据融资退潮是主要拖累，企业中
长期贷款边际改善但居民端连续净偿还。货币端 M1 同比
回升至 4.4%、M2 同比降至 7.1%，剪刀差收敛至 2.7 个百
分点，资金活化出现阶段性修复信号，但其中包含低基数
因素，尚不能确认企业内生扩张重启。综合来看，10月数
据延续了"降速提质"的存量验证逻辑：财政托底使总量读
数企稳，但私人部门融资需求仍偏弱，政策重心正由"宽
松力度"转向"传导效率"。

国贸期货  樊梦真 
从业资格证号： F3035483 
投资咨询证号： Z0014706

### Page 2

交易咨询业务资格：证监许可【2012】31号

➢ 事件： 
2026 年 11 月 13 日，中国人民银行发布《2026 年 10 月金融统计数据报告》。10 月末社会融资规模存
量 469.16 万亿元，同比增长 7.2%（前值为 7.1%）；前十个月社融增量累计 28.61 万亿元，比上年同期少
2.29 万亿元，10 月单月新增约 1.15 万亿元，同比多增逾 3000 亿元。信贷方面，10 月末人民币贷款余额
283.33万亿元，同比增长4.7%（前值4.8%）；前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元，10月单月少增1000
亿元，同比少增约3200亿元。10 月末 M2余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%（前值7.2%）； M1余额116.9
万亿元，同比增长 4.4%（前值3.2%）。 
图表 1：社融和信贷  图表 2：M1和 M2

资料来源：Wind、国贸期货研究院  资料来源：Wind、国贸期货研究院

➢ 点评： 
 总量：社融增速止跌微升，但改善几乎全部来自政府债单极支撑 
10 月社融存量同比 7.2%，结束此前连续两个月的回落，单月新增社融约 1.15 万亿元，同比多增逾
3000亿元。从结构归因看，改善动力高度集中于政府债券：10月政府债净融资约1.18-1.19万亿元，占当
月社融增量比重超过100%，剔除政府债后的社融增速降至约5.2%，私人部门融资需求仍在走弱。前十个月
直接融资合计占社融增量约 53%，较前三季度进一步提升，超过贷款占比约 14 个百分点，融资结构由信贷
主导转向"政府债+直接融资"双支柱的格局进一步固化。从资金价格信号看，10 月企业新发放贷款加权平
均利率约2.9%，同比低约0.2个百分点；个人住房贷款利率约3.1%，与上年同期基本持平，金融供给端条
件仍处历史宽松区间。因此，总量读数的企稳更多反映财政发力和直接融资渠道畅通，而非实体自发融资

需求的反转。 
图表 3：社融的季节性  图表 4：社融&M2&资金价格

 结构：政府债独撑，企业债修复、非标分化 
10 月社融分项呈"政府债独撑、信贷拖累、直接融资补位"格局。政府债券净融资约 1.18 万亿元、同
比多增约 7000 亿元，是社融同比多增的唯一正向来源；社融口径人民币贷款减少约 5800 亿元、同比多减
约 5700 亿元，是最主要拖累。直接融资方面，企业债券净融资约 3400 亿元、同比多增约 950 亿元，在债
券利率低位环境下，债券对信贷的替代效应继续显现；股票融资约 350 亿元、同比略少增。表外融资边际
改善：委托贷款增加约1860亿元、同比多增约200亿元，与政策性金融工具通过委托贷款通道投放有关；
未贴现银行承兑汇票减少约 1700 亿元但同比少减约 1200 亿元；信托贷款减少约 415 亿元。综合看，10 月
社融的结构特征可概括为"财政补位、票据退潮"——政府信用扩张对冲了银行表内信贷的季节性收缩，但
直接融资对信贷的替代并非线性推进，单月节奏仍存在波动。

 信贷：票据退潮拖累单月负增，企业中长期边际修复、居民持续收缩 
10 月新增人民币贷款-1000 亿元，为继 4 月、7 月之后年内第三次单月负增，同比少增约 3200 亿元。
结构拆解显示，票据融资同比少增约8000亿元，是信贷负增的主因——前期集中投放的票据陆续到期，叠
加监管对票据冲量约束强化，银行"以票充贷"空间明显压缩。剔除票据后，企业贷款实际新增约 1500 亿
元、同比多增约3000亿元，其中企业中长期贷款新增约1600亿元、同比多增约1300亿元，可能初步体现
新型政策性金融工具与专项债配套的融资支持，但改善幅度有限、持续性仍待观察。 
居民端则延续净偿还：10 月住户贷款减少约 4300 亿元，同比多减约 700 亿元；短期贷款减少约 2700
亿元，中长期贷款减少约 1560 亿元、同比多减约 900 亿元。前三季度累计住户贷款已减少 1.03 万亿元，
10 月单月再减约 4300 亿元，前 10 月累计减少 1.46万亿元，而去年同期为净增加 1.10万亿元，居民部门
去杠杆已从慢变量演化为持续性硬读数，与 10月 30城商品房成交面积同比降幅扩大至 18%相互印证。

 货币供应：M1 回升至4.4%、M2 降至 7.1% 
10 月末 M1 同比 4.4%，较 9 月回升1.2 个百分点；M2同比7.1%，较 9月再降 0.3个百分点；M2-M1 剪
刀差由 4.0 个百分点收窄至 2.7 个百分点，为年内较优水平。M1 的回升包含两方面因素：一方面，去年同
期基数偏低对读数形成支撑，不能完全归因为企业经营活跃度实质改善；另一方面，财政资金拨付提速、
企业活期存款边际回流、以及政策性金融工具落地带来的项目资金活化，确实对 M1 形成正向拉动。M2 继
续回落则与信贷派生偏弱、非银存款波动、财政存款淤积有关——10月财政性存款前十个月累计增加3.03
万亿元，政府债募集资金属阶段性滞留国库，尚未完全转化为一般性存款。 
存款结构显示，住户存款前十个月增加 8.47 万亿元，居民端储蓄倾向仍强；企业存款增加 1.91 万亿
元，活化程度边际改善但仍偏谨慎。综合判断，M1 的修复是积极信号，但需结合后续订单、PPI 与项目开

分析师介绍 
樊梦真：国贸期货宏观金融首席分析师，毕业于中国科学院数学与系统科学研究院。国债、外汇研究员，
专注于宏观金融领域的研究，善于利用跨专业背景对宏观经济、外汇、利率及其衍生品进行分析，熟练运
用数理工具为客户提供专业服务与交易策略。曾获期货日报“最佳宏观金融期货分析师”称号。

伺服器、平板、網通設備等終端產品，以及

鴻海 (2317 TT) 
規模效益顯現

永豐觀點 
AI 伺服器為成長主軸，規模效益顯現，觀察政策長期對 AI 資料中心建置的影響。 
 投資評價與建議 
研究部維持鴻海買進建議：鴻海 3Q26 財報、4Q26 展望優於預期。2027 年維持
以 AI 伺服器為成長主軸，公司表示 2027 年 AI 伺服器產業需求仍大於供給，關鍵
零組件持續吃緊，惟仍需觀察期中選舉後，政策長期對大型企業 AI 資料中心建置
時程的變化。研究部認為鴻海在提高市占率、營收規模放大的同時體現獲利率效
益、獲利金額擴大以及營運資金運用效率。研究部預估鴻海 2026 年/2027 年稅後
EPS 19.33 元/25.51 元。維持鴻海買進建議，目標價 356 元(14x2027FEPS)。

ESG 評析 
鴻海企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2025 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。

### Page 2

2

鴻海為專業 ODM/EMS 廠商，產品涵蓋手機、伺服器、平板、網通設備等終端產
品，以及各類電子零組件如連接器、連接線等，旗下重要子公司包含工業富聯
(601138 SH)、富智康(2038 HK)及鴻騰精密(6088 HK)，近年公司開始積極投入研
發資源在電動車、數位醫療、機器人、AI、半導體、5G 等領域。主要客戶為 Apple、
HP、Dell、Sony、Cisco、北美四大 CSP 等。3Q26 產品營收占比：消費暨智能
29%、雲端網路 54%、電腦終端 12%、元件及其他 5%。 
3Q26 財報優於預期：鴻海 3Q26 毛利率 6.02%，營益率 3.83%，優於預估的
5.93%、3.77%。鴻海營收規模放大，營運槓桿效益顯現。業外收入貢獻 33 億元，
稅後淨利 775 億元，稅後 EPS 5.51 元，優於預估 5.10 元。3Q26 鴻海存貨金額
1.5 兆，季增 10%，年增 27%，存貨天數 45.7 天，季減 3 天，年減 4 天。存貨狀
況尚屬健康。 
4Q26 展望略優於預期：鴻海展望 4Q26 營運，AI 伺服器出貨量季增高雙位數(部
分專案採 Consignment 商業模式)，預期 ASIC 及 GPU 出貨續增，Vera Rubin AI
伺服器機櫃單價跳升，推估雲端網路營收季增約 15%。消費性產品 iPhone 維持
強勁出貨動能，PC 需求則持續疲弱季減。研究部略為上修 4Q26 營收預估季增
17%，稅後 EPS 上修至 5.99 元。 
AI 伺服器為鴻海營運增長動能 留意長期資料中心建置影響性：因應客戶 AI 建置
需求，鴻海持續支出投入產能，主要客戶為美系大型 CSP、AI 模型商及 Neocloud，
降低資金及庫存風險。並透過與客戶改成採用 Consignment 商業模式，提高營運
資金效率，並優化獲利率表現。研究部預期鴻海維持 Vera Rubin 的主要供應商角
色，整體市占率維持達 40%以上，同時鴻海 2027 年將增加美系 CSP AI ASIC 伺
服器機櫃供應，為 2027 年營運增長動能。公司表示 2027 年 AI 伺服器產業需求
仍大於供給，關鍵零組件持續吃緊，惟仍需觀察期中選舉後，政策長期對大型企業
AI 資料中心建置時程的變化。研究部認為鴻海在提高市占率、營收規模放大的同
時體現獲利率效益、獲利金額擴大以及營運資金運用效率。 
綜合以上，研究部預估 2026 年營收 11.2 兆，年增 38%，毛利率 6.0%，營益率
3.7%，稅後淨利 2,710 億，年增 43%，稅後 EPS 19.33 元。2027 年營收 15.5 兆
元，年增 38%，毛利率 5.4%，營益率 3.4%，稅後淨利 3,562 億元，年增 31%，
稅後 EPS 25.51 元。

Date: 2026-11-16

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: MPI hosted its investor meeting on 16 November. 3Q26 
revenue was TWD5.9bn (+12.8% QoQ), 3.2% below our forecast but 2.1% 
above the Bloomberg consensus. Gross margin rose 3.3pp QoQ to 61.7%, 
beating our/consensus forecasts by 2.2pp, driven by a better product mix. 
Operating margin reached 38.6% (+4.1pp QoQ), 1.4/3.2pp above 
our/consensus forecasts. Management guided for 4Q26E/2027E revenue 
growth of 5-10% QoQ/50% YoY, with gross margin of c.62%/above 60%, 
respectively. We believe the company’s guidance is conservative and 
forecast 4Q26/2027 revenue to be TWD6.7bn/TWD37.7bn, with gross 
margin of 61.8%/61.5% and operating margin of 39.5%/39.9%, 
respectively.

What’s the impact: Probe card capacity expansion on track. MPI 
maintained its capacity expansion plan, targeting monthly VPC/MEMS 
capacities of 2.0m/3.5m probes by end-2026 and 2.5m/6.0m by end-2027. 
Management plans to release its final 2027 expansion plan by end-
December, with potential upside to current targets. Demand remains solid, 
with capacity still insufficient to meet customer needs and no signs of order 
cuts, per management. We believe testing intensity is the key driver of 
sector growth and we expect MPI's diversified customer base to limit the 
impact of order fluctuations from any single customer or project.

CPO contribution likely to accelerate in 2028. Although the market 
seems conservative about its CPO ramp-up, the company claimed that 
Insertion 2 qualification at a tier-1 customer remains on track, with 
qualification results expected by end-December and potential mass 
production orders in 1H27. For Insertion 3, qualification is progressing with 
multiple customers, with potential production orders in 1H27. Given 
qualification lead times, we forecast the CPO testing machine to contribute 
5-13% of revenue over 2027-28, aligned with management’s expectation 
that CPO will contribute a double-digit % in 2028.

What we recommend: We lower our 2026-28E EPS by 6-13% to reflect 
higher opex and cut our 4-quarter-forward target PER multiple from 65x to 
45x to reflect recent macro uncertainties, peer derating and moderating 
earnings growth. As a result, we lower our 12-month TP to TWD5,625 (from 
TWD8,415), while reaffirming our Buy (1) rating. Our target multiple implies 
a c.0.6 PEG based on our 2026-28E EPS CAGR of 73%. Given its 
diversified customer base and continued AI/HPC demand growth, we view 
current levels as offering an attractive risk-reward profile.

How we differ: Our 2027-28E EPS are 4-5% below the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more conservative opex assumptions.

16 November 2026

Solid fundamentals; attractive entry post derating

➢ Capacity expansion on track; AI/HPC demand remains intact 
➢ CPO contribution likely to become more significant in 2028  
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) rating; lowering 12-month TP to TWD5,625

Source: Daiwa forecasts

Company profile 
Founded in Hsinchu in 1995, MPI is a leading global probe card supplier offering CPC, VPC and MEMS 
solutions, alongside photonics, semiconductor and thermal testing equipment. Growth is driven by 
expanding AI/HPC demand, rising MEMS adoption, capacity expansion, deeper vertical integration and 
emerging opportunities in CPO testing.

矽力*-KY (6415 TT) 
明年營收成長性可望達 30%

永豐觀點 
資料中心與車載為成長主軸，兩者總計明年營收比重可望達 45%。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年稅後 EPS 10.92 元(原預估 11.95 元)，2027
年稅後 EPS 15.32 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營運拓展，研究部
維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

ESG 評析 
矽力*-KY 企業永續評鑑整體為 B 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面
向平均得分分別為 B+等與 B 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2023 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學)

專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用

### Page 2

2

矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠：矽力杰成立於 02/2008，為電源管理 IC 設計公司，
產品線包括 DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置
與 LED 背光驅動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。矽力杰 3Q26 產品終端應用主要區
分為五大領域，包括消費電子(如平板電腦、STB 與 TV)34%、工業應用(如 LED 照
明、伺服器與安防設備等)37%、資訊產品(如筆電)7%與網路通訊(如路由器、手機
等)7%、車載 15%。國際電源管理晶片設計同業與矽力杰產品類似者為 TI、Maxim、
ON Semi 與 ADI，台系類似的參與廠商則包括致新、茂達等。 
3Q26 營業費用較高：矽力杰公布 3Q26 營收 72.59 億元(+21%QoQ，+52%YoY)，
毛利率 47.58%，稅後獲利 9.09 億元(-42%QoQ，+34%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.31 元，
財報不如預期，主要是營業費用規模偏高。矽力杰 3Q26 財報細項指標如下：(1) 
營收 72.59 億元(+21%QoQ，+52%YoY)，此係來自資料中心訂單加持、車載上
升(新款晶片推出與市占率提升)、消費電子季增 14%(即便市場需求疲弱，但因公
司新款晶片推出，故反而仍能向上)、資通訊產品下滑(因記憶體報價上漲，推高終
端售價而影響消費力道)。(2) 毛利率由 2Q26 的 47.03%微增至 47.42%，符合公
司原預期。(3) 營業費用 24.78 億元，季增達 19%，主要是銷售費用、資料中心應
用研發費用、3Q26 應屆畢業生招募人力增加。(4) 業外損失約 1100 萬元，相較
2Q26 業外收益 9.58 億元出現明顯差異，主要是前期有加特蘭微電子的再評價增
值利益產生。(5) 存貨 70.36 億元，季增 23%，存貨天數 150 天，達到歷史上緣位
置，公司表示係因封測供給吃緊，導致封測交期拉長(部分達到六個月)，因此 wafer bank
囤積在封測廠，導致庫存水位增加。

### Page 3

3

4Q26 營收持平到小增，略有不如預期：矽力杰預期(1) 4Q26 營收持平到小增(研
究部原預估 QoQ+5%~6%)，其中營運動能仍以資料中心為成長主軸，不過消費
電子市況相當疲弱(公司推測可能與客戶因應原物料與供應鏈不確定性，而提前出
貨，導致 4Q26 拉貨動能驟減)，包括手機與 TV 均顯弱勢。(2) 毛利率隨產品結構
變化、Gen4 製程平台滲透率提升至 20%，使得毛利率應能優於 3Q26 水準。
(3)4Q26~1H27 營業費用建議以 3Q26 的水準計算。研究部預估矽力杰 4Q26 營
收 74.29 億元(+2%QoQ，+38%YoY)，毛利率 47.84%，稅後獲利 10.5 億元
(+16%QoQ，+30%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.67 元。 
2027 年營收成長性落於 YoY+20%~+30%上緣，反映成長力道延續：矽力杰維持
預期資料中心、車載與為主要成長推手(兩者 2027 年營收總計 YoY+>50%)，並給
予更明確的營收比重目標值：(1) 資料中心(包括主板/企業級 SSD/光模組)目前營
收比重 17%，2027 年底預計可達 25%。公司資料中心三大項目比重依序為光通
訊>SSD>MB，其中光通訊與 SSD 個別均為 mid-to-high single digit，MB 比重
雖較低，不過主板業務(包括控制晶片與 SPS)的新款晶片目前客戶均已處驗證中，
預計 2027 年將放量(公司表是北美客戶的 SPS 需求快於中國)。公司表示儘管近期
資料中心有諸多因素干擾，不過客戶針對明年訂單需求仍相當穩固。(2) 車載營收
比重可望由現階段 15%，攀升至 2027 年的 20%(原預估 15%~20%)，主要動能
來自於 content value 提升(由 200~200USD 往 270~300USD 邁進，係來自 MCU
與高電壓/大電流晶片出貨)、中國電動車的海外銷售攀升。(3) 公司期待隨 Gen4
製程平台 2027 年滲透率達 50%、Gen5 製程平台量產與產品結構持續往資料中
心、車載布局，對 2027 年毛利率>50%有信心。不過研究部認為矽力杰過往毛利
率容易受到存貨損失調整(因庫齡政策)影響，又製造成本上漲壓力延續，故雖預估
2027 年毛利率趨勢向上，但上升的幅度以較保守的態度推估。

維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年營收 255.51 億元(+36%YoY)，毛利率
47.6%，稅後獲利 42.98 億元(+72%YoY)，稅後 EPS 10.92 元(原預估 11.95 元)，
並預估 2027 年營收 338.3 億元(+32%YoY)，毛利率 48.98%，稅後獲利 60.32 億
元(+40%YoY)，稅後 EPS 15.32 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營
運拓展，研究部維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적HD현대중공업(329180) 우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
우려 감안해도 충분히 매력적 
2026-10-14

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 820,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 366,500 원 
상승여력: 123.7%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 및 환율 영향으로 시장 예상치 하회 예상 
Key: 4Q26 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 계약 수주 모멘텀 기대 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 4 행정 엔진&FDC 으로 주가 상승여력 
충분. 매수

3Q26 Preview: 컨센서스 하회하는 실적 기록 예상 
3Q26 연결 매출액 6조 464억원(+36.9% YoY, -4.5% QoQ), 영업이익 9,230억
원(+65.6% YoY, -11.2% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(1조 70억원)를 하회하는 실적 기
록할 것으로 추정한다. 이전 분기 대비 중대재해 및 하계휴가, 그리고 추석 연휴에 따른 
조업일수 감소 효과와 더불어 큰 폭의 환율 하락 영향(약 500 억원) 때문이다. 홍범도
함 화재 및 임단협 관련 일회성 비용은 이번 분기가 아닌 4Q 에 반영될 것으로 예상한
다. 해양 부문에서 트리온 FPU 프로젝트 C/O 에 따른 일부 환입 발생한 것으로 추정
되며, 해양 도크에서 컨테이너선 선체 블록 일부 건조로 고정비 감소효과 일부 상쇄 중
이다. 다만 올해 연내 혹은 내년 초 추가 해양 수주 없을 시 고정비 상승 우려 확대될 
전망이다. 동사 올해 상선 수주 목표(114.7 억불)를 이미 초과하는 수주 실적 기록한 상
황이며, 고마진 선종인 LNGC 및 VLGC/VLAC 중심으로 수주했기 때문에 ‘28~’29
년 구간 경쟁사 대비 뚜렷한 선종 믹스 효과를 기대한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 82 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 97 만원에서 82
만원으로 하향한다. 중국의 공격적인 캐파 증설 지속과 더불어 중국 대형 조선사들의 
글로벌 선주가 발주한 LNGC 물량 및 원가 경쟁력 확대에 따른 상선 부문의 멀티플 
조정이 불가피하다는 판단이다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클
과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 FDC에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지
속되고 있는 중으로 파악된다. 올해 4 분기부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 부문의 본격적
인 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 전망이며, 해당 모멘텀에 따른 주가 상승
여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)를 유지한다.

2026.11.04   
LG에너지솔루션 
(373220)

ESS 수주 모멘텀 이어진다 Company Brief

3Q26 실적, 원-달러 환율 하락에도 시장 기대치 대폭 상회 
동사의 3Q26 실적은 매출 9.6조원(+59% YoY, +28% QoQ), 영업이익 7,560억원 
(+26% YoY, +568% QoQ)으로 원/달러 환율 하락에도 불구하고 시장기대치(매출 
8.8조원, 영업이익 3,217억원)를 크게 상회했다. 당사는 Stellantis와의 캐나다 
합작관계 종료에 따른 보상금 등을 포함해 약 4,250억원의 일회성 이익이 반영된 
것으로 추정한다. 이를 제외한 영업이익은 약 3,310억원으로, 시장기대치에 대체로 
부합한 것으로 판단된다. AMPC는 미국 ESS 생산량 증가와 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동에 
힘입어 전분기 대비 약 73% 증가한 4,169억원을 기록했다. 사업부문별로는 ESS의 
실적 개선이 두드러졌다. 상반기 배터리 패키징 병목으로 이연됐던 물량 출하가 
집중되면서 ESS 출하량은 전분기 대비 60% 가량 증가한 것으로 추정된다. 자동차 
전지도 유럽향 물량 회복과 GM 얼티엄셀즈 재가동으로 매출이 전분기 대비 약 
33%로 증가했으며, 고정비 부담 완화에 따라 적자폭도 뚜렷하게 축소된 것으로 
판단된다. 소형전지는 주요 고객사인 Tesla의 판매 호조에 따른 출하 증가로 매출 
회복과 수익성을 뒷받침한 것으로 보인다. 
4Q26 ESS 중심의 수익성 개선과 수주 모멘텀 지속될 전망 
미국 ESS 시장은 신재생 에너지 확대와 AI 데이터센터의 전력 수요 증가에 힘입어 
빠르게 성장하고 있다. 특히 정책 환경도 비PFE 공급망을 확보한 업체에 유리하게 
작용하고 있다. 동사는 3Q26 실적 컨퍼런스 콜에서 ESS 수주잔고가 약 
160GWh이며, 2028년까지 캐파의 대부분에 대응하는 물량을 확보했다고 밝혔다. 
또한 현재 다수의 고객사들과 2028년 이후 공급 물량을 협의하고 있어 연말까지 
추가적인 수주 모멘텀이 이어질 것으로 예상된다. 이를 바탕으로 4Q26에도 동사의 
본업 실적은 ESS 중심의 개선세를 이어갈 전망이다. 전분기에 반영된 대규모 일회성 
이익의 기저효과로 영업이익은 감소하겠지만, 이를 제외하면 출하량 증가와 가동률 
상승에 따른 점진적인 수익성 개선이 예상된다. 수익성 중심의 사업 효율화를 위해 
소형 IT용 파우치 사업을 축소하고 있다는 점도 중장기 손익 개선에 긍정적이다. 이를 
반영한 4Q26 매출과 영업이익은 각각 약 10.5조원(+63% YoY, +9% QoQ), 
4,150억원(흑자전환 YoY, -45% QoQ)으로 추정된다. 
매수 투자의견과 목표주가 유지 
동사에 대한 매수 투자의견과 목표주가를 유지한다. 북미 ESS 수요 증가와 공급망 
재편에 따른 신규 수주 확대가 중장기 실적 성장을 뒷받침할 전망이다. 특히 ESS용 
생산라인 전환과 가동률 상승이 본격화되면 매출 증가에 더해 고정비 부담 완화와 
AMPC 수혜 확대가 맞물리면서 수익성도 개선될 것으로 예상된다. ESS의 역할이 
신재생 에너지 연계용 저장장치를 넘어 AI 데이터센터의 안정적인 전력 공급을 
지원하는 핵심 인프라로 확대되고 있다는 점에도 주목할 필요가 있다. 이에 따라 
동사의 밸류에이션 역시 기존 EV 중심의 평가에서 벗어나 전력 인프라 산업의 
성장성을 반영하는 방향으로 재평가되어야 한다. 업종 내 실적 개선 가시성과 정책 
수혜가 뚜렷하고, 4Q26 신규 수주에 따른 주가 상승 가능성도 높다는 점에서 동사에 
대한 비중 확대 전략이 유효하다는 판단이다. 
Buy (Maintain)

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

종목추천 투자등급

STRONG RESILIENCE TO EXTERNAL SHOCKS
, 
SOLID 
MACROECONOMIC FUNDAMENTALS 
◼ 
 Over the past 
 two years
 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its 
macroeconomic fundamentals have remained solid.  
◼ 
 In FY2025/2026  
(ended in March 2026)
 , India was hit by a sharp rise in US tariffs. However, thanks to supportive fiscal 
and monetary policies, 
 real GDP  
grew by 7.8% in FY2026, placing India among the fastest
 -
 growing Asian economies.  
◼ 
 For the current fiscal year, the Indian economy is facing rising energy prices 
 (
due to 
 the conflict in the Middle East and 
increased food prices 
 because 
 of 
an 
unfavourable monsoon
 ) 
as well as 
 monetary tightening. Nevertheless, despite these 
new challenges, real GDP growth is expected to exceed 7%.  
◼ 
 India is currently in a stronger position than it was in 2022 to cope with the new energy shock. The fiscal leeway to support  
the economy has increased with the decline in the fiscal deficit, and inflation is more contained.  
◼ 
 Although 
 rising  
energy prices have put pressure on external accounts, FX reserves 
 remain  
sufficient to cover the country’s 
short
 -
 term external financing needs. 
 Yet, 
downward pressures on the rupee have been 
 significant 
 and 
could persist in the 
short term
 . 
In 
H1 
2026, India’s external accounts withstood the rise in energy prices fairly well
 , notably  
thanks to the sharp increase 
in workers’ remittances. 
 T
he 
widening of the trade deficit was primarily due to the substantial increase in machinery and 
equipment imports 
 while t
 he oil trade deficit increased 
 only 
moderately, as the rise in energy prices was offset by a decline 
in import volumes. 
 However, 
in H2 2026, the oil trade deficit is expected to widen significantly.  
Additionally, large portfolio outflows have exacerbated downward pressure on the rupee, necessitating interventions by 
the central bank in foreign exchange markets and the adoption of regulatory measures to support the currency. Although 
these measures hav
 e led to a rebound in the rupee (at least temporarily), inflationary pressures, volatile portfolio 
investments, and low net foreign direct investments are 
 expected to continue 
 to put pressure on the currency.  
◼ 
 D
ownward pressures on the rupee 
 and 
 rising bond yields will reduce the government's 
 already limited 
 fiscal capacity to 
increase public investment and 
 support economic development. However, this will not undermine its ability to refinance 
its debt, given its safe structure.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector is sufficiently robust to cope with the expected rise in credit risks.

STRONG ECONOMIC GROWTH DESPITE 
STRUCTURAL CONSTRAINTS 
◼ 
 In October 2026, the IMF revised upward 
 India’s  
medium
 -
 term growth 
 forecast 
 from 6.5% to 6.8%  
per year
 . Nonetheless, 
to achieve such strong growth performance, 
 India will have to adapt to the challenges posed by artificial intelligence  
(AI)
, 
while its infrastructure and education system are not prepared for it.  
◼ 
 Moreover, India’s development remains highly uneven. 
 The middle class is still modest
 . This is explained by a large 
informal economy, heavy concentration of employment in low value
 -
 added sectors such as agriculture, low participation 
rates among women and youth, and limited industrial development. All this, in turn, reflects India’s weak i
 ntegration into 
global value chains.  
◼ 
 India faces two key structural challenges: i
 ) adapting the services sector to AI and ii) accelerating industrial development. 
Even with progress in sectors such as smartphones and pharmaceuticals, India’s industrial base remains too small to 
absorb its vast labour force. 
 Labo
u
r market 
 rigidities  
and the mismatch between training and business needs are holding 
back both the creation of quality jobs and industrial development.  
◼ 
 Low net foreign direct investment is a 
 major  
 obstacle. While 
 India attracts 
 new 
 FDI, retention is weak
 : 
 despite 
improvements in logistics, governance issues and regulatory barriers have 
 led 
 disinvestments to triple over the past five 
years.  
◼ 
 India 
 may fail in completing its 
 transition to a higher
 -
 value, more industrialised and inclusive economy if reforms do not 
accelerate 
 significantly
 . Structural change
 s 
could lag behind pressures from its rapidly growing population and 
 fast 
global 
technological advances
 . 
02 November 2026

### Page 3

EcoInsight

economic
 -
 research.bnpparibas.com

3

INDIA
’S COMPLEX POSITION 
IN INTERNATIONAL TRADE 
Low 
attractiveness  
due to unfavourable business environment and limited integration in global value chains  
◼ 
 Despite very strong growth, India's attractiveness is low relative to the size of its economy. The stock of foreign direct 
investment stood at only 15% of GDP in Q2 2026 compared to 52% of GDP in Vietnam and around 67% of GDP in Thailand 
and Malaysia.  
◼ 
 This lack of appeal can be attributed to 
 India’s still unfavo
 u
rable 
 business environment and 
 its 
 limited integration into 
global value chains. India’s global market share remain
 s 
 low (1.9% of total 
 world 
 exports  
 of goods
 )
, 
 especially in 
comparison with that of China (16.1% of total exports).  
◼ 
 India has not benefited from the AI boom
 , 
since its exports of AI
 -
 related goods remain modest (0.5% of its total exports)
 . 
However, 
 it has benefited from the “China +1” strategy adopted by 
 international 
 companies seeking to diversify their 
production sources. Smartphone exports by India have increased 6.3
 -
 fold over the past five years and accounted for almost 
6% of global exports in 2025 compared to less than 1% five year
 s 
earlier.  
To 
increase its a
 ttractiveness and develop its industrial sector, India has 
 multiplied F
 ree 
T
rade 
 A
greements  
◼ 
 Over the past 18 months, 
 India  
has signed a growing number of free trade agreements 
 – 
with the European Free Trade 
Association in October 2025, the United Kingdom in July 2025, Oman in December 2025, and the European Union in January 
2026
. This 
 should help to develop the industrial base and demonstrates the Modi government's commitment to 
accelerating India's integration into global trade.  
The sectors most likely to benefit from the recent trade agreement with the EU are electronics and semiconductors, 
pharmaceuticals and chemical
 s
, textile and renewable energy technologies. But expanding trade agreements without deep 
domestic improvements in the business environment, governance, education, and infrastructure will be ineffective. India 
will continue to struggle with investment reten
 tion. 
◼ 
 The US
 -
 India bilateral trade agreement has not yet been concluded. The two sides reached a framework for an interim 
Agreement in February 2026, under which the US would apply an 18% reciprocal tariff on Indian goods, with further 
reductions subject to the 
 successful conclusion of the agreement. As of early October, negotiations 
 are 
stalled. Key sticking 
points include market access, particularly 
 in 
India’s sensitive agricultural sector.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE

Non transfer risk   
India’s external accounts are solid with a low external debt, moderate current account deficit and large FX 
reserves. 
 India’s net debtor position reached only 5.9% of GDP in Q2 2026. In the short term, external accounts 
are expected to deteriorate 
 given 
the rise in energy prices while downward pressures on the rupee should remain 
high.  
◼ 
 External accounts have remained solid 
 despite recent adverse shocks
 . 
 In 2025, external accounts remained solid 
despite the significant increase in US tariffs. In 
 H1 
 2026, they held up well against rising energy prices. Although the 
current account deficit is expected to widen for the current fiscal year as a whole, it should remain contained, and foreign 
exchange reserves should be more than sufficient to cover the co
 untry’s short
 -
 term financing needs. The 
 major  
causes 
for concern are the 
 structurally 
 low level of foreign direct investment (although it is rising) and the increased reliance on 
portfolio investment, which exposes the currency to greater volatility.  
◼ 
 Structural vulnerability to energy price shock
 . 
India is structurally vulnerable to an energy price shock as its 
 oil and gas  
trade deficit is relatively high (2.8% of GDP in 2025). However, in H1 2026, the current account withstood fairly well the 
rise in energy prices especially thanks to the sharp increase in workers’ remittances  
(+0.7 pp to 3.7% of GDP)
 . The trade 
deficit widened significantly but principally because of the strong increase in machinery and equipment imports rather

### Page 4

EcoInsight

economic
 -
 research.bnpparibas.com

4

than to the rise in the oil bill. The 
 energy 
 trade deficit increased moderately as the rise in energy prices was offset by the 
decline in imported volumes.  
However, the trade deficit is expected to widen further during the rest of the year with the significant rise in oil prices, 
and 
 the current account deficit 
 is projected  
to 
increase to 
 1.
6
% of GDP for FY 2026/2027, compared to 0.7% of GDP for FY 
2025/2026. 
 This is 
 still modest and slightly lower than during the previous energy shock  
in 2022
 .  
◼ 
 Low net FDIs.  
Although rising in H1 2026 (+0.2 point of GDP), net FDI inflows remained low at only 0.5% of GDP  
and did 
do not cover the entire current account deficit. In fact, t
 he country’s reliance on portfolio investment  
inflow
 s to meet its 
external financing needs has gradually increased over the past five years.  
◼ 
 FX reserves 
 remain very comfortable  
and represent a solid buffer protecting against external shocks
 . 
Officially, 
 they 
reached  
USD591 bn at end
 -
 October, covering 6.5 months of imports. 
 However, a
 djusted for the net short
 -
 term forward 
position (
 –
USD
200 bn, ~50% from RBI’s dollar swaps for non
 -
 resident deposits), "usable" reserves cover
 ed 
only 4.6 months 
of imports, a historic low. 
 Nonetheless, t
 his constraint is future
 -
 oriented, as much of the non
 -
 resident deposit dollars are 
due for repayment in 3
 –
5 years.  
Additionally, 
 FX 
 reserves still exceed external debt service (
 estimated at 
 USD 63 bn by 
the central bank).  
◼ 
 External debt is low (20.8% of GDP and 87.1% of total exports in Q2 2026). 
 Banks accounted for the largest share of 
external debt (30.9% of total), 
 followed by 
 non
-
 financial corporations (29.8% of total). The general government
 ’s 
 share 
reached 22.3%.   
◼ 
 Currency risk
 : 
 India is vulnerable to capital outflows and is very exposed to change in US monetary policy. 
 Its 
performance will continue to depend on energy prices and the spread between India’s and the US government bond yields.

India's current account widened significantly

Sovereign risk  
Public finances are structurally fragile 
 and fiscal policy room is narrow, 
 as India has one of the lowest tax bases 
in Asia and one of the highest debt interest burdens (second only to Pakistan). Nonetheless, the debt refinancing 
risk is limited given 
 its 
sound 
 profile 
 (
debt 
is 
denominated in domestic currency and held by domestic investors 
with a long maturity).

◼ 
 In FY2026 (ended in March 2026), public finances continued to improve. 
 The central government’s fiscal deficit 
narrowed to 4.4% of GDP from 5% of GDP the previous year. 
 However, t
 his reduction stemmed primarily from a significant 
rise in dividends from the central bank. 
 G
overnment revenues remained low at  
9.6% of GDP, with tax revenues stagnant 
at 
7.6% of GDP. Besides, debt
 -
 servicing costs climbed to a peak of  
3.6% of GDP, equivalent to  
37.6% of total government 
revenue
s
. 
The fiscal position is stronger than during the 2022 energy crisis, with the general government deficit estimated 
at 
7.8% of GDP  
in FY2026, compared to  
9.5% 
in FY2021/2022.  
◼ 
 For FY2027, the initial budget targets a slight reduction in the central 
 government 
 deficit, to 4.3% of GDP. This goal may 
however 
 prove challenging due to rising subsidies amid the energy shock.  
To cushion households from the shock, the government initially 
 kept 
petrol prices 
 unchanged
 . Refining firms absorbed the 
financial burden, partially offset by lower excise duties on petroleum products. Only after regional elections in mid
 -
 May 
did the government allow a modest increase in pump prices to reflect higher international 
 prices
 . 
The government has 
also increased subsidies  
to offset the impact of rising fertiliser prices on farmers.  
Revenue losses  
—
driven by  
lower excise 
duties, reduced dividends from state
 -
 owned enterprises, and the economic slowdown
 — 
could reach  
0.5% of GDP
 , while 
the cost of 
 fertiliser subsidies 
 could 
 rise from  
0.4% to 0.8% of GDP.  
To reduce the risk of fiscal slippage, the government 
has: i) raised taxes on fuel exports, ii) capped household diesel purchases; and iii) planned cuts to certain expenditures.  
◼ 
 While the primary deficit reduction  
 target may prove difficult to achieve, 
 the surge in government bond yields  
 is 
another source of severe concern
 . 
Over the first ten months of 2026
 , 
10
-
 year bond yields rose by 80 basis points, 
 and 
further increase  
is 
expected due to 
 the central bank’s 
 monetary tightening. A  
100
-
 bp 
rise in interest rates  
could push debt  
servicing costs above  
40% of 
fiscal 
 revenue by 2029
 . This is expected to 
 increase the debt
 -
 to
-
 GDP ratio 
 gradually
 .

Strong pressures on the rupee and government bond yields

Source: RBI, BNP Paribas

◼ 
 Despite the high public debt level (84.6% of GDP in FY2026), refinancing risks remain moderate. 
 The debt structure is 
relatively safe, and the government benefits from access to 
 large 
 domestic savings. In Q2 2026, 87.8% of central 
government debt was domestic
 , and 
 70.5% was market debt
 . 
It is 
 almost entirely held by domestic financial institutions 
and denominated in local currency. 
 R
efinancing risks are limited as most debt is long term (the average maturity of central 
government debt is 13.8 years). T
 -
 bills (with a maturity of less than one year) account for only 4.2% of central government 
debt. Foreign investors held only 3.1% of 
 Treasury 
 securities in Q2 2026. 
 Finally,  
borrowings 
 in foreign currency 
 accounted 
for only 3.0% of total general government debt in FY2025/2026 (
 i.e. 
2.6% of GDP).

Systemic credit risk  
The banking sector has strengthened over the past five years
 , 
as illustrated by the improvement in 
 asset  
quality 
and capital buffers. The main sources of credit risks are rising household debt, the rise in energy prices, the 
unfavourable monsoon and monetary tightening.  
◼ 
 The quality of assets has improved significantly. 
 In Q1 2026, the banking sector’s non
 -
 performing loan (NPL) ratio fell 
to a multi
 -
 decade low of 1.8%. For public banks  
(
54.4% of total loans
 )
, the NPL ratio declined to 2.1%.  
◼ 
 Capital buffers are strong. 
 While 
loan loss provisions remained modest at 75.6% of NPLs, capital buffers stayed strong. 
The Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR) rose to 17.7% in Q1 2026, up from 17.4% a year earlier. From April 2027, Indian banks 
will adopt a new Expected Credit Loss (ECL) framewo
 rk, requiring earlier provisioning for potential loan losses. In the 
short term, higher provisioning requirements could push up lending rates and tighten credit conditions 
 while 
 triggering 
capital raising or targeted government recapitalisation. 
 I
n the medium term, the reform could strengthen banking  
sector 
resilience through earlier recognition of credit risks and more robust loss buffers.  
◼ 
 Banks’ 
 liquidity 
 is 
 comfortable
 . 
It deteriorated slightly in H1 2026 because credit growth outpaced deposit growth
 ; t
he 
liquidity coverage ratio decli
 ned to 
a still solid 
 124% 
and 
the loan
 -
 to
-
 deposit ratio increased to 83% in Q2 2026 from 79.1% 
a year earlier. RBI interventions to support the rupee 
 also 
 drained liquidity
 . Moreover
 , the deposit mix shifted from low
 -
cost current and savings accounts (CASA) to higher
 -
 cost term deposits, pressuring net interest margins. However, since 
June 2026, liquidity has improved significantly due to RBI measures to attract foreign
 -
 currency non
 -
 re
sident (FCNR) 
deposits.  
◼ 
 Profitability remains satisfactory. 
 It 
 has declined since the start of the year, primarily due to narrowing net interest 
margins, but remains satisfactory. In Q2 2026, 
 the 
 return on assets (ROA) and return on equity (ROE) stood at 1.6% and 
13.0%, respectively (IMF data).  
◼ 
 P
rivate
 -
 sector  
 debt 
 is moderate
 , 
 but its increase 
 needs to be monitored.  
 It stood 
 at 105.1% of GDP in Q1 2026 (BIS  
data
), compared to 100.1% five years earlier
 , which is 
 lower than in other emerging Asian economies.  
Non
-
 financial corporat
 e 
debt was 56% of GDP in Q1 2026. Listed firms’ financial positions continued to strengthen amid 
surging profits, improving their ability to service debt. In Q2 2026, the 
 average 
 interest coverage ratio (ICR) rose to 7.4, up 
from 6.1 a year earlier. However, the share of companies with an ICR below 1 (insufficient earnings to cover interest costs) 
increased to 18.5%. Stress remain
 s 
 concentrated in micro
 -
 enterprises, agriculture, and energy
 -
 intensive sectors (
 e.g.
, 
power, steel, cement, aviation), which are 
 the 
most exposed to oil
 -
 price shocks.  
Household leverage requires monitoring. Although still moderate, household debt increased to 49.1% of GDP in Q1 2026, 
up from 42.7% five years earlier, driven largely by non
 -
 housing retail loans (58.4% of total borrowing).  
◼ 
 Monetary 
 policy 
 tightening. 
 Due to rising  
inflation (+5.5% year
 -
 on
-
 year in September) and downward pressure on the 
rupee, the RBI raised its policy rate by 25 basis points to 5.5% in October 2026 under a "calibrated tightening" stance.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector should remain resilient  
 despite rising risks in the short term. 
 Due to rising energy prices, a poor 
monsoon, and monetary tightening, asset quality is expected to deteriorate, particularly for low
 -
 income borrowers, micro, 
and small enterprises. 
 E
arly warning signs are emerging
 , with some 
 borrowers starting to miss payments. The stress is 
concentrated in unsecured retail loans  
(
which account for over half of fresh retail slippages
 )
. 
This could lead to new 
 NPLs 
over the coming quarters, especially as higher rates and inflation squeeze repayment capacity. Additionally, in the near 
term, capital inflows may remain weak, 
 and 
 liquidity and funding pressures could intensify.

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T

CTCP VÀNG BẠC ĐÁ QUÝ PHÚ NHUẬN (PNJ)  
Cập nhật ĐHĐCĐ bất thường 21/10/2026

Tái cơ cấu để xử lý áp lực thanh khoản  
— ĐHĐCĐ bất thường PNJ ngày 21/10/2026 đã thông qua điều chỉnh kế hoạch kinh 
doanh và phương án phát hành riêng lẻ để bổ sung vốn tái cấu trúc. Ban lãnh đạo đặt 
ra các vấn đề trọng yếu là đáp ứng nhu cầu thanh khoản và chuẩn bị nguồn lực để thực 
hiện các cam kết thu đổi đã tích lũy trong quá khứ. Bên cạnh đó, các cổ đông tham dự 
cũng đặt các câu hỏi liên quan đến tình hình hoạt động qua các điểm chính (1) dòng 
tiền hoạt động kinh doanh; (2) diễn biến thị trường sau khi PNJ áp dụng chính sách thu 
đổi mới; (3) hiệu quả hoạt động sau tái cấu trúc.

Nội dung chính tại đại hội 
— Theo Ban lãnh đạo, thông tin liên quan đến P-Lab từ đầu tháng 7 khiến khách hàng gia 
tăng bán lại sản phẩm. Tổng giá trị thu đổi và mua lại trong tháng đạt gần 6,000 tỷ VND, 
so với khoảng 7,000 tỷ VND trong cả năm 2025. Theo BCTC riêng, nguồn tiền khả dụng 
giảm từ khoảng 3,700 tỷ VND cuối Q2/2026 xuống 770 tỷ VND cuối Q3/2026, đã bao 
gồm khoản vay 700 tỷ VND từ Chủ tịch HĐQT. Tỷ trọng kim cương và trang sức kim 
cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% lên 52%. 
— Q3/2026 ghi nhận lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND trước phần tăng trích lập dự phòng. Để giảm 
áp lực thanh toán, PNJ kéo dài thời gian chi trả đối với kim cương lên 120 ngày từ tháng 
07/2026. Thay đổi chính sách cũng ảnh hưởng đến niềm tin khách hàng và sức mua, cụ 
thể doanh thu và lợi nhuận gộp Q3/2026 đạt lần lượt 6,995/609 tỷ VND, giảm 14%/62% 
YoY. Chi phí bán hàng và quản lý giảm 13% YoY chưa đủ bù đắp mức giảm lợi nhuận 
gộp, khiến PNJ lỗ sau thuế 258 tỷ VND, so với lãi 496 tỷ VND trong Q3/2025.

Biểu đồ 1. Kết quả kinh doanh theo quý  Biểu đồ 2. Diễn biến doanh thu thuần/ngày và doanh thu bán 
lẻ/ngày theo quý

### Page 2

F I R S T  T O  M A R K E T 
 — PNJ nâng giả định tỷ lệ thu đổi trên giá trị thu đổi còn lại lên 80%, so với 28.8% đối với 
nhóm sản phẩm giai đoạn 2020–2026 trong ước tính tại BCTC bán niên 2026. Tổng dự 
phòng tổn thất dự kiến tăng từ 2,267 tỷ VND lên 7,071 tỷ VND. Kế hoạch điều chỉnh ghi 
nhận doanh thu 39,057 tỷ VND, lỗ trước thuế 6,059 tỷ VND và lỗ sau thuế 6,271 tỷ VND. 
Theo Ban lãnh đạo, LNST loại trừ dự phòng ước khoảng 800 tỷ VND, chủ yếu nhờ kết 
quả Q1/2026. Khoản dự phòng phản ánh tổn thất ước tính cho toàn bộ kịch bản, không 
đồng nghĩa toàn bộ giá trị sẽ được chi trả ngay trong kỳ. 
— Giá trị cam kết thu đổi tích lũy đối với kim cương và trang sức kim cương tại cuối 
Q3/2026 được PNJ ước tính khoảng 21,000 tỷ VND. Với hàng đã bán, doanh nghiệp dự 
kiến chuyển đổi quyền lợi khách hàng sang sản phẩm và các chương trình ưu đãi. Với 
sản phẩm bán mới, chính sách thu đổi định hướng theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm 
giao dịch, thay cho tỷ lệ cố định trên giá trị hóa đơn, nhằm hạn chế tiếp tục tích lũy 
nghĩa vụ tiềm tàng trong tương lai theo cơ chế cũ.

Phương án bổ sung vốn và tái cơ cấu hoạt động kinh doanh 
— Đại hội thông qua phương án chào bán tối đa 550 triệu cổ phiếu. Nguồn vốn dự kiến 
phân bổ 60% cho kế hoạch kinh doanh gắn với điều chỉnh chính sách thu đổi, 20% bổ 
sung vốn lưu động và phần còn lại bổ sung vốn lưu động và/hoặc trả vay ngắn hạn. 
Nhu cầu vốn Q4/2026 và Q1/2027 lần lượt khoảng 3,100 tỷ VND và 2,800 tỷ VND. Giá và 
khối lượng phát hành thực tế chưa được xác định tại đại hội.  
— Doanh nghiệp dự kiến đóng 25–30 cửa hàng và mở khoảng 10 cửa hàng (có chọn lọc) 
trong năm 2026, tương ứng giảm ròng 15–20 cửa hàng; đồng thời đặt mục tiêu giảm 
20–30% chi phí nhân sự. Cổ tức tiền mặt năm 2026 được điều chỉnh về 0%; cổ tức 2025 
về 10% mệnh giá và đã chi trả. 
— 100% mẫu kim cương được AIGS và Vinacontrol kiểm định độc lập là kim cương thiên 
nhiên, phù hợp thông số 4C. PNJ dự kiến trang bị máy kiểm định tại các cửa hàng trọng 
điểm để khách hàng kiểm tra trực tiếp.

Đánh giá của KBSV 
— KBSV cho rằng huy động vốn từ phát hành riêng lẻ là phương án phù hợp ở thời điểm 
hiện tại trong bối cảnh lãi suất tăng cao, giúp PNJ cân đối thanh khoản và duy trì hoạt 
động liên tục. Tuy nhiên, với khối lượng phát hành lớn và chưa có phương án chi tiết, 
doanh nghiệp sẽ cần thêm thời gian để hoàn thành phương án phát hành và cân đối 
giữa lợi ích của cổ đông và doanh nghiệp.  
— Nhu cầu yếu tại Trung Quốc và cạnh tranh từ kim cương nuôi cấy giá thấp tiếp tục gây 
áp lực lên giá kim cương tự nhiên, khiến PNJ gặp khó khăn trong việc tiêu thụ tồn kho 
nhóm kim cương  và gia tăng tổn thất khi mua lại theo chính sách cũ. Thực tế, tỷ trọng 
kim cương và trang sức kim cương trong tổng tồn kho tăng từ 41% cuối Q2/2026 lên 
52% cuối Q3/2026, với giá trị ước khoảng 9,000 tỷ VND. KBSV cho rằng cần theo dõi (1) 
nhu cầu thị trường trong giai đoạn cuối năm; (2) tốc độ giảm tồn kho và dòng tiền thu 
về từ bán hàng; và (3) giá trị khách hàng bán lại. Diễn biến các yếu tố này sẽ quyết định 
khả năng thu hồi vốn, mức độ phục hồi biên lợi nhuận và nhu cầu trích lập bổ sung 
trong quá trình xử lý. 
— Ước tính bán niên sử dụng tỷ lệ tổn thất 20.3% đối với hàng bán trong giai đoạn 2020–
2026 dự kiến được thu mua lại, thấp hơn mức 28.9% trên các giao dịch mua lại thực tế 
từ 01/07–03/09/2026. Vì vậy, bên cạnh giả định thu đổi 80%, mức tổn thất khi xử lý 
hàng mua lại là yếu tố quan trọng cần theo dõi. Việc chuyển hạch toán dự phòng từ 
SG&A sang giá vốn tại kỳ soát xét bán niên cũng cho thấy áp lực biên lợi nhuận bị co 
hẹp trong quá trình tái cấu trúc về mặt kế toán.

Thông báo ngày 01/08/2026 Chính sách áp dụng từ 30/09/2026 Định hướng tại ĐHĐCĐ ngày 21/10/2026 
Theo tỷ lệ trên giá trị hóa đơn, tùy chủng loại, kích thước và 
điều kiện sản phẩm Không công bố Theo giá thị trường tại thời điểm thu mua đối với sản phẩm bán mới 
Thanh toán thành 5 đợt trong vòng 120 ngày Duy trì lộ trình 120 ngày đối với kim cương rời và trang sức kim cương Không công bố 
Áp dụng lộ trình thanh toán 120 ngày T+2 đối với vàng miếng, nhẫn khoen và một số sản phẩm vàng; T+7 đối với các nhóm trang sức được quy định Không công bố 
Chuyển đổi một phần hoặc toàn bộ giá trị mua lại sang sản 
phẩm PNJ

kim cương được chuyển sang kim cương rời, trang sức 
kim cương/vỏ/đá màu/ngọc trai

phẩm hiện hữu, kết hợp tiền mặt để xử lý cam kết thu đổi 
và tồn kho 
Ưu đãi bổ sung 3%–5%, tùy nhóm hàng và tỷ lệ giá trị 
chuyển đổi Không công bố Không công bố

hoặc nhận tiền tại cửa hàng chuyên trách, từ 15h–17h

chuyên trách Không công bố

Document 342: FX Daily_ Higher US real rates dominate

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Higher US real rates dominate.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-19
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

US short-dated real interest rates are pushing back to their
highest levels since 2023 as markets get the message that the
Fed is ready to apply the brakes. These high real rates are
sucking funds out of asset classes like precious metals and are
triggering a broader fallout across emerging market currencies.
There is no reason to ﬁght this trend near term

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole and Frantisek Taborsky
Higher US real rates are sucking funds into the dollar
USD: Two-year real swap rates are now
2.4%
The market has got the message. We understand the Fed needs to
take rates higher to get inﬂation back to target. And we are now
seeing US two-year real rates (swap rates less the zero-coupon
inﬂation swap) pushing above 2.40%. The last time rates were at
this level was in 2023. Higher real rates are especially negative for
zero-yielding precious metals and one of the reasons why gold and
silver and linked currencies like the South African rand and Thai
baht are under pressure.

### Page 2

On the Fed, we have seen further comments from Chris Waller
alongside the release of the October FOMC minutes over the last 24
hours. Waller has now turned ultra-hawkish and is pushing for
more than a reversal of the 75bp 'insurance' rate cuts seen last
year. However, he may be out on his own here. According to the
FOMC minutes, only a 'couple' of Fed voters favoured a faster pace
of tightening at the October meeting and the market now looks
quite settled on pricing 25bp hikes in December, January and next

summer. When could these views be challenged? Perhaps not until
energy-softened November CPI data is released on 10 December.
This all seems a very positive story for the dollar then. There has
also been speculation that foreign interest in Anthropic's
blockbuster IPO has seen some demand for the dollar this week. If
that is the case, that dollar demand could wane into tomorrow
once IPO allocations are settled. Yet there are good reasons for the
dollar to be at current levels, and DXY advancing to the 104.60 area

remains our bias for now.
For today, all eyes will be on how Anthropic (ANT) trades on its ﬁrst
day, while low initial jobless claims should point to a solid US jobs
market.
Chris Turner
EUR: Macro resilience, but swamped by
the dollar
EUR/USD is consolidating under 1.10 as the strong dollar dominates
across the board. EUR/USD price action masks ongoing resilience on
the macro side, where business conﬁdence remains relatively high
and better-than-expected eurozone growth rates this year are

projected to continue into 2027. As above, it is hard to see the
strong dollar trend turning before mid-December, and we think
EUR/USD still risks a drop to the 1.0880 area.
A light data calendar today just sees the release of September
eurozone current account data. High energy prices in September
may have weighed further on the surplus, although Germany

Chris Turner
ZAR: SARB to leave rates on hold today
The South African Reserve Bank meets to set interest rates today.

Having hiked interest rates to 7.25% in September, no one is
expecting a further hike today. However, money markets do price
another 25bp hike in early 2027. Unlike Banxico, the SARB has been
responsive to the inﬂation shock and we see much greater scope
for the rand to bounce back than Mexico's peso. See a more in-
depth discussion on the rand's prospects in our 2027 FX Outlook.
Chris Turner
MXN: Lack of rate protection comes
home to roost
At one point, USD/MXN was nearly 3% higher on the day as large

parts of the emerging market complex succumbed to the strong
dollar. We heavily discussed the risk of peso underperformance in
our 2027 Latam FX Outlook, given that Banxico had cut the policy
rate spread protection over the Fed to very slim levels. We see no
reason for USD/MXN to turn lower until the broader dollar trend
turns. And, ultimately, that may not be until the second quarter of
next year, when US inﬂation is substantially falling.
Chris Turner

熱門議題
► 黃金價格歷經第二季修整，近日呈現大幅反
彈走勢，主要係因美國期中選舉後，市場憂
心AI產業監管將趨嚴，加上晶片與科技股遭
遇大幅修正，促使部分資金轉往黃金等防禦
型資產尋求避險。
► 根據世界黃金協會最新報告，第3季全球央行
淨買進321公噸黃金，較第二季明顯回升，年
增率達42%，創下自2024年第四季以來的單
季新高，前3季累計買進金額更是高達675公
噸，顯示黃金儲備已經列入全球央行必需資
產配置而且比重維持穩定增長態勢。
► 長期以來黃金具有抗通膨特性，觀察黃金與
聯邦基金利率走勢，從2020年之後，即便全
球歷經2次升息循環，黃金價格仍呈現穩健上
揚走勢，反應其抗通膨保值特性。此外，美
國財政赤字壓力難消，恐令美元長期購買力
被稀釋， 加 速 全 球 央 行 去 美 元 化 ， 有 鑑 於
此，建議黃金納入資產配置。

資料來源：Bloomberg，Factset，凱基證券整理；註：右上產業回報相關性為各產業過去3年之每週報酬相關性
► 醫療類股於美國第三季財報展現強勁的獲利
韌性，實際獲利優於預期程度為主要產業中
表現最佳，背後主要受惠大型製藥企業(如：
禮來、再生元製藥、吉利德科學)在減重藥、
癌症治療及創新藥領域之需求持續增長，帶
動整體產業獲利表現優於市場預期。
► 期中選舉後醫療政策輪廓逐漸明朗。民主黨
主張支持擴大醫療保險覆蓋、延續平價醫療
法案補貼以及緩和醫療補助計劃(Medicaid)
削減，有助提升醫療服務使用率；雖藥價談
判政策恐帶來部分定價壓力，但整體政策主
張仍對創新藥、生技研發及醫療保險產業具
潛在政策支持效果。
► 綜上，在AI投資回報率面臨市場檢驗之際，
市場資金持續尋求防禦性配置。醫療保健類
股與資訊科技類股相關性偏低，有助分散投
資組合對單一AI題材依賴，加上第三季獲利
動能轉強、創新藥需求穩健及產業能見度提
升，後續有望持續受惠資金輪動效應。

財報與政策題材雙利多，醫療類股成市場新寵兒

降低藥價 依《通膨削減法案》框架，擴大醫療 補助計劃(Medicaid)藥價談判適用範圍 傾向透過最惠國定價機制來降低藥 價 兩黨均主張降低藥價 製藥企業面臨定價壓力
美國本土製造 透過研發補助、稅收優惠及產業政策 鼓勵藥廠回流美國生產 透過關稅、供應鏈重組及企業承諾 推動製藥產能回流 兩黨均支持醫藥供應鏈自主化 有利本土藥廠及原料藥供應鏈投資
醫療保險覆蓋
支持延長《平價醫療法案》補貼、擴
大醫療補助計劃(Medicaid)覆蓋範圍，
並緩和該補助計劃的削減
傾向由州政府及市場機制主導醫療
體系，限制聯邦政府支出
醫療的可負擔性為兩黨政策共同焦點，有助
增加就醫人口及處方藥使用量，支撐製藥、
醫療服務及保險業長期需求

投資焦點必需消費
► 短期而言，檢視各類股在最近股災表現，美
國成長股、資訊科技、與通訊服務等高波動
度類股，表現明顯落後大盤，其中，資訊科
技與通訊服務波動度最大、跌幅最重，主要
係因美股科技巨頭財報不如預期、負自由現
金流量，疊加期中選舉後民主黨恐緊縮AI基
礎建設進程及資本支出見頂等不確定因素，
令市場 擔憂 導致 資 金流出 ；並轉 進核 心消
費、健康護理、防禦股、公用事業等防禦性
產業，報酬相對較穩健，受市場資金青睞。
► 長期而言，從2008迄今，核心消費類股長期
表現穩健上漲。與主要指數相比，面對近月
AI科技股修正，同期間費半大跌18%、那斯
達克跌幅6%、標普跌幅1%，而核心消費類
股大漲13%，反應其低波動、抗震韌性。
► 整體而言，從2015年至今，歷經2次通膨與
升息循環，核心消費受影響性低，有鑑於資
產配置角度，建議短期跟隨資金流向配置核
心消費股，提高投組抗震韌性。
必需消費長期表現穩健、抗通膨、低波動避險資金流入，必需消費類股單月表現最佳

配置必需消費類股，提高投組抗震韌性

股市
 隨著美國期中選舉結束，美伊衝突可望降溫，市場關注焦點將回歸基本面，惟AI資料中心建設進程
或將受到州政府緊縮政策而造成評價面壓力增添股價波動。短期可布局防禦股、分散配置於不同國
家與產業，增強抗震力、降低市場波動影響，長期而言，AI從全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高
的品質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨勢不變。
 歐、日股可分散配置，利率走升有利銀行股。此外，隨財政政策支持，歐洲國防軍工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，AI從
全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高的品
質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨
勢不變，非AI部分可關注必需消費產業、
醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 隨資金湧入，公債殖利率回落，惟根據最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升
息空間，立場偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨發展。建議以中短天期債券投資為主，可逢利率走升
時布局進行鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引

力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 聯準會12月份會議升息態度偏鷹，美元高檔整理。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期偏鷹，日圓弱勢盤整。
美元高檔整理，日圓弱勢盤整
商品
 美國期中選舉結束，預期中東地緣衝突降溫，油價從近期高點回落，短線仍偏震盪。
 去美元化支撐全球央行買盤，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
低接買盤流入，黃金低點反彈，中長期維
持偏多看法

### Page 16

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
OCTOBER CPI PREVIEW
Inflationary pressure fades as we move through year-end
17 November 2026
Key points
• October CPI to rise 0.41% with the annual 
pace lifting to 4.8%yr. The seasonally 
adjusted CPI lifts 0.7%mth, a small step 
up from the 0.6% increase in September.  
• October Trimmed Mean (TM) is set 
to gain 0.42%mth lifting the annual 
pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr. 
• There is a small downside revision 
from our October 19th estimate for 
the December quarter CPI to 1.2%qtr 
while the TM remains 0.9%qtr.  
• The December quarter will represent the 
peak in Australian inflationary pressure. 
Our March quarter CPI remains 0.4%qtr 
and the TM is unchanged at 0.7%qtr. 
• We now see TM inflation dipping below 
3% by Q3 2027 and be well within the 
RBA’s target band by end 2027 at 2.7%yr.
• Demand remains the larger driver of 
inflation, but the recent pick-up in 
trimmed mean inflation appears to 
have been largely supply-driven.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Inflation accelerated in September...
The CPI rose 0.8% in the month of September, stronger than 
Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the market’s expectation of 
0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr. The 
seasonally adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting 
some positive seasonality in the month. 
Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: auto 
fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday travel & accommodation 
(8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation 
(7.2%mth); and property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There 
were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–3.1%mth); 
household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The more important underlying measure, the Trimmed Mean 
(TM), gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and 
market expectations with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr 
(revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised 
pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this 
series.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second 
consecutive month. The share of the basket running above 
3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, while the share below 2%yr 
rose from 27% to 32%. Meanwhile, our replication of the San 
Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum index moved into 
positive territory after trending higher since April. Around 
14% of the CPI basket, on an expenditure-weighted basis, 
experienced positive inflation shocks in September, driven 
by holiday travel, fuel, and clothing & footwear. This was 
partly offset by negative shocks across 7% of the basket, 
concentrated in food & non-alcoholic beverages. For full 
analysis of the September CPI, see “ Inflation accelerating in 
the run up to Christmas”.
... but is set to peak in October.
Looking ahead to October, early indicators were mixed. The 
NAB business survey suggested firms continued to absorb 
a meaningful share of higher input costs, with purchase cost 
growth edging up to 2.4%qtr (+0.1ppt from September), while 
retail price growth slowed sharply to 0.2%qtr (–0.8ppts), 
partly reflecting some moderation in labour cost growth 
(see here). Meanwhile, consumers’ year-ahead inflation 
expectations rose to 5.3% in October, with this rate holding 
steady in November. Fuel prices provided a more pronounced 
source of inflationary pressure, with retail petrol and diesel 
prices jumping 8.7% and 5.3%, respectively, in October to 
average $2.40/l and $2.82/l as global oil prices approached a 
local peak.
October to mark the peak in inflation
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.4% (0.41% to 2dp), lifting
annual inflation to 4.8%, which we expect to mark the peak.
On a seasonally adjusted basis, we estimate a stronger 0.7%
increase (0.68% to 2dp). For the monthly trimmed mean, we

expect a 0.4% rise (0.42% to 2dp), nudging the annual pace up
to 3.7%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking a 1.0% increase
in October. While conceptually different from the quarterly
trimmed mean, this measure nonetheless provides a useful
guide to near-term underlying inflation momentum. For market
services excluding volatiles & holiday travel, we have pencilled
in a 0.3% rise, lifting annual inflation to 4.0% from 3.8%,
which had marked a ten-month low.

Transport is expected to make the largest contribution
(+0.35ppts), almost entirely reflecting auto fuel (+0.32ppts).
Smaller contributions are expected from clothing & footwear
(+0.06ppts), household goods & services (+0.05ppts), and
meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The main offset comes
from recreation & culture (–0.18ppts), driven entirely by
holiday travel (–0.18ppts), while fruit & vegetables, utilities
and several other components provide only marginal offsets.

Key details for the month of October
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to rise 0.2% in
October, lifting annual inflation to 3.3%. Strength in the month
is concentrated in meals out & takeaway, where we expect
prices to rise 0.6%mth and 4.3%yr. We have allowed for a
greater-than-usual degree of price resetting as businesses
adjust to the card surcharging ban.
Housing inflation is expected to rise 0.1% in October, with the
annual pace increasing to 6.2% reflecting base effects from

electricity. Across the major components:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.2%mth and 3.5%yr, with the
late-September indexation of the Commonwealth Rent
Assistance flowing through more broadly in October. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate is stronger at 0.4%mth.
• New dwelling inflation has been a key source of downside
surprise in recent months. We expect a 0.2%mth rise
in October, which would see the annual pace moderate
to 4.5%. The downturn in established home prices has

deepened into year-end (see here), which should weigh on
new dwelling inflation over the months ahead. Our current
estimates point to a trough of around 1.3%yr by late-2027.
• Maintenance & repair costs are expected rise a solid
0.9%mth, taking annual inflation to 3.5%, while gas & other
household fuels provide the main offset, falling 2.5%mth.
Transport is expected to be the main source of upward
pressure in October, rising 2.9%mth and lifting to 11.6%yr.

This largely reflects an 8.2%mth jump in auto fuel prices
following the rise in global oil prices through the month. Motor
vehicle prices and spare parts are also expected to increase, by
0.4%mth and 1.6%mth respectively.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Recreation & culture is expected to provide the key offset, 
falling 1.5%mth, driven by a 3.0%mth decline in holiday travel. 
We also expect some pull-back across components exposed 
to the AI trade, including audio, visual & computing equipment 
(–0.2%mth) and games, toys & hobbies (–0.3%mth).
Underlying inflation holds momentum
As noted above, our October TM estimate is also 0.4%mth, a step 
up from 0.3% in September taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 
3.6%yr and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 
4.0%yr, the fastest pace in the short history of the series. Rents 
and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
Upside risks to October baseline ...
We view the risks around the October CPI outcome as slightly 
tilted to the upside. Our estimates already allow for a greater 
degree of price resetting in areas such as meals out & takeaway 
and personal and household services, but there is a risk that this 
adjustment proves broader than we have assumed. Increases in 
fuel levy surcharges in early October are another source of upside 
risk, including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post, with 
the latter nearly doubling its surcharge between September and 
October. The key downside risk remains housing, particularly 
new dwelling prices, which have surprised to the downside in 
recent months and could continue to do so as the housing market 
downturn deepens. Trade notifications data also point to some 
further downside risk, with builders on average announcing 
smaller price changes than in prior months.
... but momentum to ease in November ...
We have a preliminary estimate for the November CPI of 
–0.1%mth which will see the annual pace ease back to 4.6%yr. 
Auto fuel moved lower through November to be down –2.1% in 
the month with seasonal falls in garments, health, furniture & 
household appliances and holiday travel offsetting some strength 
in food, housing, communication and financial services. 
Seasonally adjusted the CPI is estimated to lift 0.1% in November, 
highlighting the seasonal weakness in the month, while our TM 
estimate is for a 0.2% lift seeing the annual pace ease a touch to 
3.6%yr while the six month annualised pace drops from 4.2%yr 
to 3.9%yr. This moderation is expected to continue into December 
with a preliminary TM estimate of 0.2%mth/3.6%yr. 
The following page has a table with monthly profiles for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to July 2027. By July 
2027 we now expect an annual pace of 3.3%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI while our estimate for the TM is 3.0%yr. 
More significantly, the six month annualised pace of the TM is 
estimated to be down to 2.7%yr by June 2027, well within the 
RBA’s target band. We have also included tables breaking down 
the monthly components of the CPI through the December and 
March quarters. 
... with December quarter now the peak ...
Our December quarter CPI estimate has been revised down 
slightly to 1.2%qtr, a modest 0.05ppt revision, with the annual 
pace lifting from 3.9%yr to 4.6%yr. The seasonally adjusted

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
CPI gains 1.4% in the December quarter highlighting normally
December is a seasonally soft quarter. Our TM estimate for
Q3 has a small downward revision of 0.05ppt to 0.85% at two
decimal place but this rounds up to our published estimate of
0.9%qtr. For now that suggests downside risk for our TM

estimate as getting the quarter to round to 0.8% is much
easier than getting it to round up to 1.0% . We also note
auto fuel prices falling faster than expected which may not
only result in downside risks to out CPI estimate but also our
TM estimate due to less pass through pressure.
... with inflation moderating through 2027 ...
Westpac’s preliminary March quarter CPI estimate is just 0.4%.
Driving most of this outcome is the 7.7% decline in autofuel

(–0.29ppt) and a 3.8% fall in holiday travel (–0.23ppt) being
offset by rising food (1.0%, 0.18ppt), housing (0.7%, 0.15ppt),
health (2.6%, 0.18ppt) and education (3.5%, 0.17ppt). The
March quarter does not have a lot in the way of net seasonally
with the seasonally adjusted estimate up 0.5%. Our underlying
TM measure of inflation is estimated to rise 0.7% in the March
quarter taking the annual pace down to 3.5% while the six
month annualised pace dips from 3.8%yr to 3.2%yr.

... as a disinflationary impulse kicks in.
We have for some time expected auto fuel prices to peak in
October, 240 cpl for petrol and 282 cpl for diesel, then to
trend lower from there. Our November estimates are currently
sitting on 237 cpl and 279 cpl respectively trending down to
186 cpl for petrol and 196 cpl for diesel by December 2027.
This represents a 12.7% fall in auto fuel through the first half
of 2027 subtracting 0.42ppt off the CPI over the same period

of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the
auto fuels profile please see: “Persistent energy pressures
have further to run”. At this stage we have not adjusted our
Brent forecast in line with the current correction underway
(see the November Market Outlook for more details why) as
we expect this to be a volatile ride. Should the current correc-
tion continue, auto fuel will represent a larger drag on the CPI
and potentially the Trimmed Mean.

Critical to our expected moderation in core inflation is a step
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab-
lished home prices. From a 1.0%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we
see dwelling price increasing by 0.4% in Q4 2026, and then
0.5% in Q1 and 0.4%qtr in Q2 before dropping to 0.2%qtr in
Q3 and Q4. This will see dwelling price inflation in the CPI
drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 2.3%yr by June 2027 then

down to 1.3%yr by December 2027.
At least to the end of this year there is still a high degree of
uncertainty about how much businesses will be able to pass
through upstream cost pressures. The latest NAB business sur-
vey continues to highlight robust input price pressure but also

that firms are struggling to pass on those increases.
For businesses, it is almost a now or never opportunity to raise
prices. We expect that through 2027, as energy prices ease
and the domestic economy slows under the weight of higher
interest rates and falling established home prices, households
will be much more resistant to price increases especially if dis-
cretionary household spending softens by more than expected.
That said, these headwinds may be at least partially offset by

the stronger starting point for household incomes, which has
proven more resilient than previously anticipated, and a possi-
ble bounce back in energy prices due to renewed conflict in the
Middle East.
Supply or demand driven inflation?
Inflation has steadily drifted away from the RBA’s target over
recent quarters. While the demand side is the larger driver of
inflation currently, most of the recent pick-up in TM inflation
appears to be from the supply side, and the more detailed

breakdown may understate how much.
There are several ways to split inflation into supply and de-
mand. One is to estimate how much of the rise a standard de-
mand-driven model can explain and attribute the rest to supply
(see here). Run through to Q3 2026, this approach puts most of
the recent pick-up in TM inflation down to supply. Without it,
trimmed mean would have risen from around 3.0%yr to 3.2%yr
over the year to Q3 2026, rather than the 2.9%yr to 3.7%yr

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
rise we actually saw. The supply contribution is moderate,
though, and well below its Covid-era peak. This analysis also
reveals that most of the disinflation through late-2023 and into
early-2025 reflected supply pressures unwinding, while de-
mand-driven inflation remained stickier, persisting above 3%yr.

Another is the San Francisco Fed approach, which the RBA has
also replicated (see here). The attribution is that if prices rise
and quantities fall, it is supply; if both rise, it is demand. We run
it to Q2 2026, as Q3 volume data are not yet available. Like the
first approach, it finds that demand accounts for most of infla-
tion (headline in this case). However, unlike the first approach,
it shows supply contributions easing a little.
A closer look at the categories classified as demand tells a

more nuanced story. Utilities, health and insurance all feature,
and their prices are largely shaped by government policy or are
not the categories that usually come to mind when people talk
about demand-driven inflation, and they are not very sensitive
to interest rates. You can look at electricity as a clear example
where prices in recent history have been set by government
policies (the introduction of, and then removal of, cost of living
rebates as well as policies to decarbonise the economy) or ex-

ternal supply shock (rising gas prices pushing up the marginal
cost of generation).
Categories more commonly thought of discretionary do show
up on the demand side, with recreation & culture (excluding
travel) and meals out & takeaway being the key ones. The
drawback of this sort of decomposition is that it labels cate-
gories where prices and quantities move together as demand,
even when this may reflect the ripple effects of a supply shock
elsewhere, a point our Chief Economist Luci Ellis detailed in a

note in 2024 (see In a world gone bananas, don’t squash the
other fruit). Some of this may be occurring now. In particular,
the analysis is likely not capturing the shift in spending towards
these categories resulting from travel disruptions associated
with the Middle East conflict (travel services itself was classi-
fied as a supply-side contributor in Q2). As travel normalises,
some of the “demand” contribution from recreation & culture
and meals out & takeaway should therefore fade over coming

quarters.
We would also acknowledge the work of Tim Toohey and his
analysis “The RBA has an identity problem”.

摘要：
SpaceX 正寻求在周四完成⾄少 320 亿美元的债务发售，为英伟达 AI 芯⽚采购提供资⾦。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠透露，本次发售包括约 60 亿美元债券，规模已从此前的 40 亿美元上调，于
周四向投资者开放认购；其余约 260 亿美元票据此前已完成配售。全部 320 亿美元债务定于周四
定价，债务由三部分组成。⾼盛与 Apollo 主导此次交易。
这笔融资的特殊之处在于结构。资⾦将通过名为 Saturn Funding Trust 的特殊⽬的载体购买英
伟达 AI 芯⽚，该载体由 Apollo ⽀持的⼀家实体管理， SpaceX 初期以约五年期租约使⽤这些芯
⽚。
债务偿付与租⾦挂钩，⽽融资安排使借款不进⼊ SpaceX 资产负债表，公司预计借此维持投资级

信⽤评级。在 AI 竞赛中，⾼评级往往被视为关键竞争优势，它意味着能更快、更多地借钱。
SpaceX 在 10 ⽉表⽰，⻉莱德、⾼盛资产管理以及 KKR 集团旗下的账⼾均是最新这笔交易的基⽯
投资者。本次发⾏依据美国证券交易委员会（ SEC ）的相关规定进⾏，可向符合条件的境内外
机构⼴泛营销，但不得⾯向部分零售投资者。
截⾄发稿， SpaceX 股价周四盘中下跌 4.5% ，英伟达股价下跌 2.3% 。

写评论
60 亿美元债券⾯向投资者公开发售，规模较早前计划增加 20 亿美元，显⽰认购需求强劲；剩余
约 260 亿美元票据在此前已直接配售给资⾦管理机构。
对这⼀结构⽽⾔，真正的关键在于会计处理：芯⽚由特殊⽬的载体持有， SpaceX 以承租⼈⾝份
出现，债务不计⼊公司资产负债表。
对⼀家正在⼤举投⼊ AI 基础设施的公司来说，这⼀点⾄关重要。科技⾏业正把数以万亿美元计的
资⾦投向数据中⼼与 AI 基础设施，并⼤量举债⽀持。评级⼀旦被下调，融资成本和融资速度都会
⽴刻受到影响。
维持⾼评级，是竞争优势
SpaceX ⾸席财务官 Bret Johnsen 在上⽉发布业绩后的投资者电话会上，强调了公司 “ 强劲的投资
级资产负债表 ” 。
这⼀表述出现在公司完成⼤规模股权与债券融资之后： SpaceX 在 6 ⽉的⾸次公开募股中售出 862

亿美元股票，数⽇后⼜定价发⾏ 250 亿美元⾼评级公司债券。
本次芯⽚融资延续了同⼀思路 —— 在不推⾼资产负债表杠杆的前提下，继续扩⼤算⼒投⼊。将
债务置于表外，使公司能够在不牺牲评级的情况下加杠杆，这对处在资本开⽀竞赛中的⼤型科
技企业尤为重要。
分销更⼴，买家更多
SEC 规则下的发⾏⽅式，让这笔债券可以触达更⼴的机构群体。
Manulife Investment Management 固收⾼级投资组合经理 Jeff Given 表⽰，这带来了⼴泛分销与
灵活性，意味着债券可能 “ 落⼊更多投资者⼿中 ” 。对于⼀笔规模超过 300 亿美元的发⾏⽽⾔，投
资者基础的宽度直接关系到定价与⼆级市场表现。

的资本结构。周四的定价结果，将检验市场对 AI 相关债务的胃⼝还能承接多⼤的供给。

Document 410: The Commodities Feed_ Copper hits six-month low; improving supply weighs on oil

Source PDF: all report pdf/The Commodities Feed_ Copper hits six-month low; improving supply weighs on oil.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Base metals remained under pressure this morning as a ﬁrmer
dollar and concerns over Chinese demand weighed on
sentiment. Oil also edged lower, with recovering supply and
rising US inventories outweighing renewed geopolitical tensions

Ewa Manthey and Warren Patterson
Copper has fallen to its lowest level since May
Energy - Recovering supply keeps pressure
on oil
Oil prices edged lower in early trading as recovering supply and
bearish US inventory data outweighed renewed geopolitical risks.
Brent was trading around $92/bbl, while WTI remained below
$85/bbl. API data showed US crude inventories rising by 5.3m
barrels last week, well above expectations for a modest 0.4m barrel
build. Gasoline stocks increased by 5.1m barrels and distillate
inventories rose by 0.4m barrels, although crude stocks at Cushing
fell by 1.7m barrels. The more closely watched EIA inventory report
is due later today.
Your privacy is important

Targeting Cookies
Save preferences Accept all

### Page 2

Supply developments are also weighing on prices. Ukrainian
attacks on Russian reﬁning infrastructure have continued, including
facilities with combined processing capacity of almost 400k b/d,
but Russian crude exports have still climbed to their highest since
late July as more barrels have been redirected overseas. Meanwhile,
OPEC+ production increased for a second consecutive month in
October to 35.6m b/d, up 1.7m b/d, led by higher Saudi output.

Russian production also increased to 8.76m b/d.
The recovery in supply is helping to ease concerns around the latest
Middle East escalation. Further disruptions around the Strait of
Hormuz remain an important upside risk, but rising OPEC+ output,
resilient Russian exports and building US inventories point to a
more comfortable supply picture. We continue to expect the oil
balance to loosen as supply recovers, limiting the scope for a
sustained rebound in prices.

Metals - Copper slips to six-month low as
base metals retreat
Copper extended losses this morning, falling to its lowest level since
May, below $13,000/t, as a stronger dollar, weaker sentiment
around AI-related assets and concerns over Chinese demand
weighed on the market.
Aluminium remained under pressure as the extreme tightness
around the November LME expiry eased. The cash-to-December
backwardation narrowed after brieﬂy reaching around $190/t on

Tuesday, but the underlying availability of exchange-deliverable
metal remains constrained. LME inventories have more than halved
this year and are at their lowest since 1990, leaving nearby spreads
susceptible to further bouts of tightness. Looking to next year,
stronger supply growth should gradually loosen the global
aluminium balance, although additional production may not
immediately translate into higher LME inventories.
Speculative positioning also points to softer sentiment across base

metals. Copper net longs fell for a second consecutive week to
33,428 lots as of 13 November, their lowest since March, while
